§  Dollar Cost Averaging (DCA) Fixed Account: 6 or 12 months 
  
Dollar Cost 
Averaging 
Purchase payments allocated to the DCA Fixed Account are 
transferred over a 6 or 12 month period into the variable 
investment opƟons you select. Any addiƟonal purchase 
payments allocated to the DCA Fixed Account made during 
the original period will be transferred over the remainder of 
that period. AlternaƟvely, you may direct transfers to or from 
any of the variable investment opƟons over a designated 
period of Ɵme on a monthly, quarterly, semi-annual or annual 
basis. 
  
Dollar cost averaging involves invesƟng a ﬁxed amount at 
regular intervals rather than a large sum all at once. This 
strategy lets you take advantage of market volaƟlity to provide 
a lower cost per unit over Ɵme. Dollar cost averaging does not 
guarantee a proﬁt or prevent a loss in declining markets. Since 
dollar cost averaging involves regular purchases, regardless of 
ﬂuctuaƟng price levels, consider your ability and willingness to 
conƟnue to make purchase payments. 
Transfers 
Unlimited. Transfer rights may be subject to restricƟons in the 
event of market Ɵming. We reserve the right to impose a $10 
charge for each transfer when transfer requests exceed 12 in a 
single contract year.

CrediƟng methods are essenƟal as they determine how interest is calculated and credited to an 
annuity based on the performance of a speciﬁed index, such as the S&P 500. Understanding 
these methods is crucial for advisors and clients alike, as they directly inﬂuence the returns 
clients can expect from their investments. 
 
Breakdown of Credit Methods 
In this email, we’ll explore the unique crediƟng methods of the Ultra 5 ﬁxed index annuity from 
Securian. Unlike many carriers that typically oﬀer a single cap rate, the Ultra 5 provides ﬁve 
disƟnct crediƟng opƟons. This ﬂexibility allows clients to navigate market condiƟons eﬀecƟvely, 
maximizing growth potenƟal while safeguarding their principal investment. 
The Ultra 5 ﬁxed index annuity from Securian exempliﬁes the importance of oﬀering more than 
just a cap rate. By providing ﬁve disƟnct crediƟng methods, it empowers clients to eﬀecƟvely

## Page 106

navigate market ﬂuctuaƟons, maximizing their growth potenƟal while securing their 
investments against downturns. 
How Credit Methods Work Together 
Understanding how these crediƟng methods interact is vital for creaƟng robust investment 
strategies. Here’s a breakdown of the ﬁve crediƟng methods available with the Ultra 5: 
Cap Rate 
The cap rate is the maximum interest that can be credited based on the index’s performance. 
For example, if the cap rate is set at 9%, and the S&P 500 rises by 10%, clients would only 
receive a credit of 9%. This ensures clients can beneﬁt from market growth while capping their 
potenƟal gains. 
Guaranteed Cap Rate 
This feature guarantees a maximum return for a speciﬁed period, such as 5 or 7 years. Clients 
can rely on this predictability, knowing exactly what they can expect in terms of returns over the 
term. 
ParƟcipaƟon Rate   
The parƟcipaƟon rate indicates how much of the index's performance clients can capitalize on. 
A 50% parƟcipaƟon rate means clients will receive half of the index's gains without any upper 
limit. This allows clients to enjoy unlimited upside potenƟal while maintaining their capital’s 
safety. 
 
Performance Trigger Rate 
With a performance trigger rate, clients are credited interest if the S&P 500 performs posiƟvely, 
even by a small margin. For instance, if the index rises by just $1, clients would receive a pre-
established interest credit, ensuring that even minor gains are rewarded. 
 
Inverse Performance Trigger Rate   
This unique feature provides a safety net in case of market declines. If the S&P 500 decreases, 
clients can sƟll receive credited interest based on a speciﬁc rate, ensuring that their investment 
grows even in unfavorable condiƟons. 
Conclusion

Client Proﬁle 
Name: Patricia   
Age: 58   
Investment Amount: $100,000   
Risk Tolerance: Moderate   
Investment Goals: Growth with downside protecƟon, income generaƟon for reƟrement, and 
preservaƟon of capital. 
Background 
Patricia is approaching reƟrement and is keen to ensure her investments not only grow but also 
protect her principal from market volaƟlity. With $100,000 available for investment, she is 
exploring opƟons that will provide both growth potenƟal and security. AŌer discussions with 
her ﬁnancial advisor, Patricia considers the Ultra 5 ﬁxed index annuity linked to the S&P 500. 
Analysis of Ultra 5 Credit Methods 
1. Cap Rate: 
Patricia is considering choosing Securian’s S&P 500 Index, which oﬀers a 9% cap rate. This 
means that regardless of how well or how poorly the S&P 500 performs, her gains are capped at 
9% with this method. For instance, if the S&P increases by 10%, she will receive a credited 
interest of 9%. However, if it only rises by 5%, she will receive a 5% credit because the actual 
performance was below the designated cap rate. 
2. Guaranteed Cap Rate: 
Patricia also considers opƟng for a lower cap rate of 7.85% to guarantee, or lock in the declared 
cap rate for 5 or 7 years unlike the 9.00% cap rate that's only locked in for 1 year. This provides 
her with a ﬁxed maximum return over this term, allowing her to predict her potenƟal earnings 
more accurately and plan her reƟrement income accordingly.

## Page 110

3. ParƟcipaƟon Rate: 
Choosing a 50% parƟcipaƟon rate allows Patricia to beneﬁt from half of the S&P’s gains without 
a cap. For instance, if the S&P rises by 20%, she would earn a credited interest of 10%, 
signiﬁcantly increasing her investment. 
4. Performance Trigger Rate: 
With a performance trigger rate of 6.5%, if the S&P rises even slightly, Patricia beneﬁts. If the 
index goes up by just $1, she will see a return of 6.5% for that year. 
5. Inverse Performance Trigger Rate: 
This rate oﬀers a safety net. If the S&P declines, Patricia will receive a credit of 12.25%. This 
feature ensures she has a cushion against market downturns, maintaining the growth of her 
investment even when markets are volaƟle. 
Scenario Analysis 
Market Performance Over 5 Years: 
1. Year 1:  
The S&P 500 increases by 5%. 
Interest Credited: $100,000 × 5% = $5,000

**Dear PaƩy,** 
I hope you're doing well! I wanted to share some insights on an investment opƟon that can help 
you achieve your ﬁnancial goals as you prepare for reƟrement.  
### Understanding Your Investment Goals 
As you approach reƟrement, it's essenƟal to ensure your investments are secure while sƟll 
providing growth potenƟal. The Ultra 5 ﬁxed index annuity oﬀers a balanced approach to meet 
these needs. 
### Why Consider the Ultra 5 Annuity? 
1. **Principal ProtecƟon**:  
   - Your iniƟal investment of $300,000 is protected. Even in a down market (like in 2022), you 
won’t lose any money. This security is crucial as you near reƟrement. 
2. **Flexible Growth OpƟons**:  
   - The Ultra 5 provides mulƟple ways to earn interest, allowing you to take advantage of market 
gains without excessive risk. Here are the three crediƟng methods: 
     - **Cap Rate (7%)**: This limits your gains to a maximum of 7%, ensuring some stability but 
potenƟally capping your growth. 
     - **Performance Trigger (6.5%)**: You earn 6.5% if the market goes up at all. This means 
even a small posiƟve gain results in good returns. 
     - **ParƟcipaƟon Rate (50%)**: You parƟcipate in half of the market’s gains. If the market 
does really well, this can lead to signiﬁcantly higher returns. 
### PotenƟal Returns 
To illustrate how these methods work, here’s how your $300,000 investment could perform 
under diﬀerent market condiƟons:

ApplicaƟon Submission

ApplicaƟon Review

Suitability Review (if applicable) 
 If your broker-dealer is not handling the suitability review, Annuity Services will review the case 
to ensure compliance.

Funds Request & Transfer Processing

Contract Issuance & Delivery

Subject: Rollovers, Transfers, and 1035 Exchanges 
Hi [Advisor’s Name], 
Here’s a quick guide to rollovers, transfers, and 1035 exchanges: 
 
Rollovers: 
Move funds between qualiﬁed plans (e.g., IRA to IRA). Submit the applicaƟon and any 
required replacement forms, ensuring consistency in dates and signatures.

## Page 149

 
Transfers: 
Funds move directly between insƟtuƟons (e.g., SEP IRA to TradiƟonal IRA). Submit the 
required forms, including the transfer form and any state-speciﬁc replacement 
documents. 
 
1035 Exchanges: 
Exchange a non-qualiﬁed annuity or life insurance policy for a new non-qualiﬁed annuity. 
Ensure “like-to-like” registraƟon (same owner and annuitant). Verify if the exisƟng 
carrier requires speciﬁc paperwork. 
Let me know if you need assistance with any part of the process or have speciﬁc quesƟons! 
Best regards,

1. Understanding Rollovers and Transfers 
Subject: Diﬀerence Between Rollovers and Transfers 
Hi [Advisor’s Name], 
Thank you for your quesƟon regarding rollovers and transfers. Here's a quick breakdown: 
 
Rollover: 
o Moves funds between qualiﬁed plans (e.g., IRA to IRA or 401(k) to IRA). 
o Can be direct (funds move directly between insƟtuƟons) or indirect (funds pass 
through the contract owner, who must reinvest within 60 days). 
 
Transfer: 
o Moves funds directly between like-qualiﬁed accounts (e.g., SEP IRA to TradiƟonal 
IRA). 
o The account type remains the same (e.g., IRA to IRA). 
Let me know if you’d like further clariﬁcaƟon or assistance with the process! 
Best regards, 
[Your Name]

completed by the broker-dealer).

Ɵme.

The per sƟrpes designaƟon allows a deceased beneﬁciary’s share to pass equally to their living

This designaƟon can be helpful in ensuring generaƟonal conƟnuity in the event of a 
beneﬁciary’s death. 
Feel free to reach out if you have quesƟons or need help draŌing these designaƟons!

AccumulaƟon: Growth PotenƟal

Decisions for Growth 
1. Leave Funds in the Annuity: 
o The contract conƟnues growing tax-deferred, oﬀering ﬂexibility for future income 
or withdrawal needs. 
o AŌer the 10-year surrender period, there are no withdrawal penalƟes, allowing

2. Adjust Investment AllocaƟons:

## Page 183

Income: ReƟrement Payout OpƟons 
Once the accumulaƟon phase ends, the client can transiƟon to generaƟng income. 
Income OpƟons AŌer Surrender Period 
1. SystemaƟc Withdrawals: 
o Flexible withdrawals allow clients to take out funds as needed. 
o Up to 10% of the contract value annually is penalty-free. Withdrawals above this 
amount may have tax implicaƟons (not penalƟes aŌer the surrender period). 
2. AnnuiƟzaƟon: 
Convert the contract value into a guaranteed income stream. OpƟons include: 
o LifeƟme Income: Payments conƟnue for life, regardless of how long the client 
lives. 
o Joint Life Income: Provides income for the client and a spouse. 
o Period Certain: Guarantees payments for a speciﬁc period, such as 10 or 20 
years. 
o Life with Refund OpƟon: Ensures any remaining balance is passed to 
beneﬁciaries. 
3. Deferred Income (Let it Grow): 
o If income isn’t immediately needed, the contract can remain deferred, allowing 
further growth without penalƟes. 
o This works well for clients looking to delay RMDs or supplement other income 
streams later in reƟrement. 
Factors to Consider When Choosing Income 
 
Income Needs: Assess how much predictable income is needed to cover reƟrement 
expenses. 
 
Market Outlook: Variable annuity payments can increase with market performance, 
while ﬁxed annuiƟzaƟon ensures stable payments. 
 
Legacy Goals: Clients aiming to leave funds for beneﬁciaries may prefer systemaƟc 
withdrawals or opƟons with a refund guarantee.

## Page 184

Post-Surrender Decisions 
AŌer the surrender period (10 years), the client has full ﬂexibility without penalƟes: 
1. Withdrawals: 
o Take out any amount without surrender charges.

contracts). 
2. Transfer to Another Product: 
o Roll over the contract value into another annuity with features beƩer aligned to 
changing goals (e.g., guaranteed income riders). 
3. Use for RMDs:

remainder to conƟnue growing. 
4. Reinvest Earnings: 
o Allocate earnings into other investments or use them for immediate ﬁnancial 
goals. 
 
Rate of Return for Growth Seekers

## Page 207

Subject:  
Funding an Annuity for Life Insurance  
 
Hi Latafath, 
It seems you're looking for a way to fund an annuity and then use it to support a life insurance 
policy aŌer 10 years. We do have a viable soluƟon, but it involves two key components.  
 
 To ensure guaranteed growth over the 10-year period, the best opƟon available would be a 
ﬁxed deferred annuity for 9 years. For instance, if you invest $500,000 in non-qualiﬁed (NQ) 
dollars, you could secure a 5% compound interest rate every year for 9 years. AŌer 9 years, the 
annuity would be completely free from surrender charges. In the 10th year, you could then 
annuiƟze the cash value with a 10 or 15-year Period Certain Single Premium Immediate Annuity 
(SPIA) to fund the life insurance. The only uncertainty lies in the SPIA rates that will be available 
10 years from now. Based on current annuity rates and the projected 5% annual compound 
interest for 10 years, here’s a summary of what a 10-year and 15-year Period Certain annuity 
would yield from a $500,000 investment:  
 
 
 
 
 - For a **10-Year Period Certain**: You could fund the life insurance with **$97,887 per year 
for 10 years** starƟng 10 years from now. - For a **15-Year Period Certain**: You could fund 
the life insurance with **$73,274 per year for 15 years** starƟng 10 years from now. AŌer 9 
years, the cash value of your investment would grow from $500,000 to **$775,664 on a 
guaranteed basis**.  
 
For your life insurance planning, it’s advisable to uƟlize the menƟoned Period Certain values 
while also seƫng aside a porƟon for taxes. AddiƟonally, consider underesƟmaƟng the income 
from the annuity since the rates could be lower in 10 years; any extra income would be a 
bonus.

## Page 208

While some may suggest using a variable, registered index-linked annuity, or an indexed 
annuity, it’s important to note that these opƟons don't guarantee growth. When planning for 
life insurance, relying on guaranteed growth ﬁgures—such as those from a ﬁxed deferred 
annuity—is crucial; underesƟmaƟng growth could lead to future issues if the life insurance 
policy is underfunded.  
 
 Lastly, one signiﬁcant advantage of annuiƟes is their tax-deferred status. All growth 
accumulates tax-deferred unƟl you begin taking distribuƟons to pay for the life insurance. When 
you annuiƟze the cash value, both the cost basis and taxable gains are distributed proporƟonally 
through what’s known as the “Exclusion RaƟo.”  
 
 Please let me know if you have any quesƟons. It might be helpful to discuss this over the phone 
if you have Ɵme today or next week. Thanks, [Your Name]

Ɵme. It’s predictable. - **ParƟcipaƟon Rate**: You get a part of how much the market goes up. 
This way, you share in the market gains. - **Performance Trigger**: Higher returns if the market 
does well. It’s a bit condiƟonal. - **Inverse Performance Trigger**: Returns based on the market 
doing the opposite of what you’d expect. It’s a unique way to earn. ### **3. Laddering 
Strategy** - **What It Is**: Spread your money across diﬀerent Ɵme periods (like 5, 7, and 10

Overview 
MulƟOpƟon Momentum is an investment-only variable annuity (IOVA), which gives 
clients the opportunity to use both variable investments and indexed account opƟons 
to help invest for the future. 
 
It also helps manage wealth to achieve long-term reƟrement goals. With one bucket 
that allows you to allocate for more growth potenƟal, and the other bucket for more 
safety, MulƟOpƟon Momentum can be customized to meet an individual’s investment 
risk comfort level. 
  
Variable Bucket (VA Bucket) 
Highlights: 
§  The opƟon to allocate up to 100% into variable subaccounts

greater)¹

HypotheƟcal Risk ReducƟon Strategy 
 
Imagine a client iniƟally invests 100% of their funds into variable subaccounts. Over Ɵme, 
they gradually reduce risk using one of these strategies:  
 
Lock In Gains Annually 
Every contract anniversary, the client transfers investment gains from the variable 
subaccounts into the ﬁxed indexed account, ensuring those gains are protected from the 
market downturns while sƟll growing with 100% downside protecƟon. 
 
Gradual Risk ReducƟon 
Instead of moving only gains, the client transfers 5% of their total contract value every 
contract anniversary into the ﬁxed indexed account, eliminaƟng fees on that porƟon while 
maintaining downside protecƟon. This strategy 
 
Gradually de-risks the porƞolio over Ɵme 
 
Keeps a porƟon of the contract secure while maintaining equity exposure 
 
Ensures tax-advantaged transfers between accounts

Fixed Annuity = A Long Term CD Where DistribuƟons are Taxed as Ordinary Income vs. 
Investment Gains and Interest Isn’t Taxed UnƟl Withdrawn 
Fixed Annuity 
 
Long crediƟng method Ɵme frames 
 
Used to lock in an interest rate that’s favorable for a period of Ɵme 
 
AlternaƟve to a CD 
 
Ability to receive interest and defer it unƟl withdrawn 
 
Ability to peel oﬀ the interest and use it to fund or provide further downside protecƟon 
with a VA 
 
Ability to be used as a guaranteed interest ﬁxed account in place of using the ﬁxed 
account subject to renewal rate risk on our ﬁxed indexed annuity 
 
Ability to ladder the interest if uncertain by distribuƟng the amount iniƟally going to be 
invested between the 4 diﬀerent Ɵme frames allowing for hedging in either case that the 
interest rate environment is x more or x less than when you originally purchased it 
  
Here's a more reﬁned and structured summary of a Fixed Indexed Annuity, making it clearer and 
more engaging: 
 
Fixed Annuity: A Smarter AlternaƟve to TradiƟonal Fixed Accounts 
A Fixed Annuity funcƟons much like a long-term CD, but with key advantages: 
 
DistribuƟons are taxed as ordinary income, rather than investment gains. 
 
Interest grows tax-deferred unƟl withdrawn. 
Key Features & Strategic Uses 
 
Locking in Favorable Interest Rates 
Secures a set rate for a ﬁxed period, providing stability in changing markets. 
 
An AlternaƟve to CDs 
Oﬀers similar security but with added tax beneﬁts and ﬂexibility.

## Page 309

 
Deferred Interest Growth 
Allows interest earnings to accumulate and be withdrawn strategically. 
 
Funding Other Investment Strategies 
Interest can be peeled oﬀ and used to enhance downside protecƟon within a VA. 
 
Guaranteed Interest OpƟon 
Can serve as a ﬁxed account alternaƟve—avoiding renewal rate risk in indexed 
annuiƟes. 
 
Laddering Strategy for Rate Hedging 
Distributes investments across mulƟple Ɵme frames to hedge against interest rate 
ﬂuctuaƟons—whether they rise or fall post-purchase.

A cap rate is a rate that determines the maximum amount that your client can be credited at the 
end, or over, a certain Ɵme period. In this case, if the S&P 500 is negaƟve, your clients receive 
0% as interest, which interprets to 100% downside protecƟon. If the S&P 500 is posiƟve and the 
actual gain percentage is below or equal to the cap rate at, the client is credited the actual gain 
percentage as interest. This

## Page 310

Let’s establish a few things that apply if we’re talking about SecureLink Ultra and take it’s S&P 
500 Index as an example that allows 100% downside protecƟon.  
  
The day that the policy is issued is the date that begins the 1 year clock to determine the 
amount your client might receive as interest. If the 1-year clock Ɵmer rings, and the S&P 500 is 
negaƟve on the 1-Year anniversary date, then your clients receive 0% as interest, which 
interprets to 100% downside protecƟon. This is the case for all of tracking methods for the S&P 
500 index on the S&P 500. Industry wide, this is usually, if not always, the case with every ﬁxed

We have an indexed version “SecureLink Chronic Illness Access” and variable version

## Page 329

Here’s a breakdown on each soluƟon: 
SecureLink Chronic Illness Access: 
 Guarantees 8% compound rollups through the client’s 85th birthday, payable as a death 
beneﬁt 
 The death beneﬁt is fully accessible for withdrawals on a cash indemnity basis if the 
client becomes chronically or terminally ill 
 The cash accumulates with a 0% ﬂoor and the S&P 500 currently is capped at 8.50% 
 No underwriƟng 
 Can be issued to clients ages 75 and under 
 Can be funded with IRA, Roth IRA, and Non-Qualiﬁed Dollars 
 This opƟon is typically selected if the client thinks they might pass away or need access 
for healthcare expenses within the next 15 years 
o Typically clients are 65-75 years old at issue for this soluƟon 
MulƟOpƟon Momentum with Premier Protector Rider: 
 Guarantees highest anniversary locks ever year through the client’s 85th birthday 
 compounds 4% every year through the client’s 85th birthday, payable as a death beneﬁt 
 The death beneﬁt is fully accessible for withdrawals on a cash indemnity basis if the 
client becomes chronically or terminally ill 
 There are zero investment restricƟons, so you can build a porƞolio as aggressive or 
conservaƟve as you desire 
 There is no underwriƟng 
 Can be funded with IRA, Roth IRA, and Non-Qualiﬁed Dollars 
 Can be issued to clients ages 70 and under 
 This opƟon is typically selected if the clients want more cash accumulaƟon and the 
ability to lock in highest anniversaries for death/chronic/terminal access 
o We’ve had clients as young as 45 and as old as 70 choose this soluƟon, typically 
for the upside for cash accumulaƟon and the ability to lock in highest 
anniversaries for legacy planning or health care expenses later in reƟrement.

## Page 428

Got it — you want to guide advisors toward this realizaƟon *without calling them out directly* 
for going all-in on RILAs. Here’s a soŌer, more conversaƟonal arƟcle that subtly leads them 
toward FIAs as the smarter play *right now*, without discrediƟng the RILA decisions they’ve 
already made: 
 
--- 
 
**When Zero Might Be the Smartest Move in the Room** 
 
Let’s be honest — the last couple years made it easy to fall in love with RILAs. Market’s been up. 
The downside buﬀer gave just enough protecƟon to make the extra upside feel worth it. And 
hey, we get it — if you locked in some solid returns, that worked. 
 
But we’re not where we were 12 or even 6 months ago. 
 
The S&P 500’s cooled oﬀ, volaƟlity’s Ɵcking up, and those RILAs? The downside buﬀers start 
looking thinner the moment a client opens their statement and sees a dip. Especially if they 
didn’t fully understand what a 10% buﬀer really meant in a 15% drawdown. 
 
Meanwhile, for the past 7 months, we’ve been talking about something diﬀerent — something 
simpler. 
 
**Fixed Indexed AnnuiƟes.** 
No downside. No fees. And with where cap rates and trigger rates are now, the upside isn’t 
something to shrug at. 
 
We’re seeing: 
- S&P 500 cap rates north of 7% 
- Trigger rates around 6–7% 
- Fixed accounts at 4%+ compounding daily 
- No annual fees eaƟng away at returns 
- 100% principal protecƟon no maƩer what the market does 
 
And if the market goes sideways? You’re sƟll locked and loaded with a 0% ﬂoor. No panic. No 
performance conversaƟons that start with, “I thought this couldn’t go down.” 
 
Here’s the real quesƟon: 
**If a client doesn’t want to lose money — and they want long-term compounding growth — is

## Page 429

a buﬀer enough? Or is a zero-loss ﬂoor exactly what they actually need right now?** 
 
We’re not saying RILAs were the wrong call. They had their moment. But if your gut’s telling you 
it might be Ɵme to pivot — or just diversify the annuity strategy a bit — you’re not alone. 
 
We’re seeing more and more advisors shiŌ back toward FIAs as the steady hand in uncertain 
markets. 
 
We’d love to walk through some updated product opƟons, show you how today’s rates stack up, 
and even build a blended strategy if that ﬁts your book beƩer. 
 
---

of this that highlights *SecureLink Ultra 5* speciﬁcally?

## Page 442

Annuity licensing and appointment 
Step 1 - Selling agreement 
To begin selling our annuiƟes, your broker dealer or agency must establish and execute a selling 
agreement with us. The selling agreement speciﬁes which annuity products are available for 
your broker dealer or agency to sell. 
We'll work closely with your broker dealer or agency to establish a solid working relaƟonship 
and clarify roles and responsibiliƟes. Once the selling agreement is established, the 
appointment process can begin. If you have quesƟons, call our annuity sales desk at 1-866-335-
7355. 
Step 2 - Geƫng appointed or contracƟng 
Financial professionals with broker dealers or agencies 
In order for you to sell our annuiƟes, you must be appointed by Minnesota Life Insurance 
Company and/or Securian Life Insurance Company. Minnesota Life issues our annuiƟes in all 
states except New York. In New York, products are issued by Securian Life, a New York 
authorized insurer. 
To sell our annuiƟes, you will need to provide the following: 
 
Complete Appointment Form 
 
Copy of your state insurance license for each state where you would like to be 
appointed. 
 
Copy of your FINRA registraƟons with current employers (if applicable). 
Forward your completed appointment form and supporƟng documents to your broker dealer or 
agency. AŌer your broker dealer or agency has authorized the appointment request, they 
should mail or fax the paperwork to Securian. 
Brokers contracƟng via IMOs 
Due to the brokerage compensaƟon structure, you need to contact your IMO for the 
appropriate appointment form, contracƟng agreement, and any supplemental forms to 
complete. Your IMO must authorize that paperwork and forward it to Securian. Once we 
execute your ﬁnal agreement, you will be provided a producer code. 
General licensing guidelines 
Any individual soliciƟng insurance products must be licensed and appointed as follows:

## Page 443

 
In the state where the broker resides, 
 
In the state where the contract owner resides, and 
 
In the state where the contract owner signs the applicaƟon. 
Pre-appointment states 
Minnesota Life issues products in the following states currently classiﬁed as a pre-appointment 
state:   
 
Pennsylvania: An appointment must be approved before ﬁrst solicitaƟon occurs for all 
products.   
 
Wisconsin: An appointment must be approved before solicitaƟon of Annuity and Long 
Term Care products (this includes Life products with Long Term Care features or Life 
products that have the LTC Agreement).  
ApplicaƟons received that are signed prior to the appointment eﬀecƟve date in these states will 
not be accepted and will be returned. 
Concurrent submission states 
All others states are considered concurrent submission states. These states do not require a 
ﬁnancial professional to be appointed prior to submiƫng the ﬁrst contract applicaƟon to the 
issuing company. 
If we receive completed appointment paperwork at the same Ɵme an applicaƟon is submiƩed 
and we determine that our primary appointment criteria has been met, we will begin our 
review of the applicaƟon and ﬁle a noƟce of appointment. 
For brokers contracƟng with us, please note that any assignee must be licensed in the assignee's 
resident state.

## Page 490

Subject: What to Expect After Your Annuity Application is Submitted  
  
Hi [Advisor's Name],  
  
I wanted to share a quick overview of what happens after your broker-dealer submits an annuity 
application to Securian, so you know exactly what to expect and how to avoid delays:  
  
Application Review & Processing 
● Once we receive the application, our Annuity Services team reviews all forms for 
completeness and accuracy.  
● If anything is Not In Good Order (NIGO), a missing requirements email will be sent to 
the case manager or financial professional.  
● To help prevent delays, here are a few common issues to double-check:  
 
Licensing & Appointment Status  
Ensure both are completed before the application is signed. If not, new signatures will be 
required.  
● Consistent Dates – The application, replacement form(s), and 1035 
Exchange/Transfer/Rollover form must all be signed with the same date.  
● Annuity Disclosure Statement – If your broker-dealer does not perform a suitability 
review, this form is required and must be fully completed.  
  
Fund Transfers & Processing 
● If funds are not included with the application, we will request them from the existing 
carrier once all paperwork is in good order.  
● If funds are included upfront, they will be applied to the contract immediately upon 
review.  
● Our Annuity New Business team follows up regularly with the existing carrier until funds 
are received.  
  
Follow-Up Timing Based on Transfer Type 
● Client-Initiated Transfers: Follow-up with you on day 30 and every 30 days thereafter.

## Page 491

● Carrier Processing Delays: If the carrier requires client action or refuses to share 
information, we’ll reach out to coordinate next steps.  
● Check Submissions: If the client submits a check, we’ll follow up every 30 days after the 
application is in good order.  
● Important: Applications are held for a maximum of 6 months if funds are not received.  
  
Contract Issuance & Delivery  
● Once funds are applied, the contract is issued and prepared for delivery.  
● Per your broker-dealer’s selling agreement, contracts are mailed to one of the following:  
○ The contract holder  
○ The financial professional  
○ TheBroker-Dealer/ IMO/ Agency  
 
After the contract is issued:  
● The contract owner will receive an Account Confirmation under separate cover.  
● You can view the confirmation online in the Statements and Confirms section of your 
Book of Business.  
  
If you have any questions about this process or want help navigating any step, please don’t 
hesitate to reach out. I’m here to help!  
  
Best regards,  
[Your Name]  
[Your Title]  
[Your Contact Information]  
Securian Financial

Hi Anthony, 
Thanks for reaching out to us here at Securian! We do have a long-term care annuity alternaƟve 
that is a ﬁxed indexed annuity.  
  
I've aƩached the product informaƟon for a more in-depth review, so please review them for 
disclosures and a complete picture!  
  
Common Clarifying QuesƟons (This one gets confusing): 
 
It's a ﬁxed indexed annuity on one side. 
 
The other side is a 8% compounding credit on the amount put in, up to a maximum of 
200%. 
 
The fees are based on the roll-up 8% value but deducted from the ﬁxed indexed amount 
side.  
 
This is an owner-driven annuity. 
 
No joint annuitants are allowed for IRAs. 
 
If the client has a permanent qualifying event, they are able to access the greater 
number between the FIA side and the 8% roll-up side. 
 
Once the client accesses the enhanced surrender value, they can use it for whatever 
they want. 
 
If they don't access or need it, the greater number between the FIA side and the 8% roll-
up side is passed on to their loved ones.

ndividual Annuity Services Producer Guide For ﬁnancial professional use only. Not for use with 
the public. This material may not be reproduced in any way where it would be accessible to the 
general public. The items described in this material may not apply for all products. This material 
is not a subsƟtute for an annuity contract or prospectus. You must review the annuity contract 
and/or prospectus for details. To the extent there are inconsistencies between this material and 
the annuity contract or prospectus, the contract or prospectus controls. This secƟon of the 
Service Guide introduces you to annuiƟes and provides informaƟon about the forms that you 
need to submit for your clients. 2 IntroducƟon to annuiƟes What is an annuity? Individual 
annuiƟes are contracts oﬀered by insurance companies that seek to guarantee income for the 
annuitant’s lifeƟme. AnnuiƟes are primarily designed to supplement income in reƟrement. 
AnnuiƟes are not investments. An annuity is a contract between you and an issuer (usually an 
insurance company). They can help to accumulate reƟrement assets and provide mulƟple ways 
for taking out reƟrement income – including an opƟon to receive a steady stream of guaranteed 
income for life. IntroducƟon to annuiƟes 2 Opening an annuity 6 Rollover/Transfer/1035 
Exchange 9 Changes and servicing the contract 12 ContribuƟons 17 Death claim procedures and 
opƟons 19 DistribuƟons 23 2 3 ClassiﬁcaƟons of AnnuiƟes Immediate AnnuiƟes - annuiƟzaƟon 
must occur no later than one year aŌer the ﬁrst annuity contribuƟon. Deferred AnnuiƟes - 
annuiƟzaƟon can occur anyƟme aŌer the ﬁrst contract anniversary. There are three types of 
AnnuiƟes: Fixed AnnuiƟes When a contract owner purchases a ﬁxed deferred annuity, funds are 
deposited into the General or Fixed Account. The contract earns an iniƟal interest rate for a 
period of Ɵme called the guarantee period. The iniƟal interest rate and guarantee period vary 
based on the contract and the product. AŌer the guarantee period, the contract earns a 
renewal interest rate. Our company guarantees that the iniƟal interest rate and the renewal 
interest rate will never be below a certain percentage. This percentage is called the guaranteed 
minimum interest rate. It is in eﬀect for the life of the contract. Renewal interest rates do not 
apply to ﬁxed immediate annuiƟes as they are annuiƟzed within one year. For immediate 
annuiƟes, the income payment amount is calculated at annuiƟzaƟon. These calculaƟons take 
into account a certain interest rate. Variable AnnuiƟes When a contract owner purchases a 
variable deferred annuity, they choose the investment funds in which they would like to apply 
their purchase payment(s). Our company does not guarantee an interest rate for the variable 
opƟons; clients assume the risk. Variable immediate annuiƟes are available. With variable 
immediate annuiƟes, the amount of the income payments will ﬂuctuate based on market 
performance. Indexed AnnuiƟes When a contract owner purchases an indexed deferred annuity, 
they are credited with interest on each contract anniversary. The interest is calculated using a 
formula based on change in an underlying index from the beginning of the contract year unƟl 
the end of the contract year. If the change in the index at the end of the year is posiƟve, the

## Page 559

client will receive interest equal to the same percentage as the increase in the index, subject to 
a maximum cap in the percentage. If the change in the index at the end of the year is negaƟve, 
the client will receive zero interest. The descripƟon above is the basic crediƟng strategy used 
with indexed annuiƟes. There are many crediƟng strategy variaƟons that exist in the 
marketplace. The variaƟons include: the length of term for the interest crediƟng, the type of 
index used with the formula calculaƟon, and the use of caps, parƟcipaƟon rates, and/or spreads 
to determine limits to the interest credited. 4 Types of AnnuiƟes (Qualiﬁed Contracts and Non-
Qualiﬁed Contracts) The contribuƟons to qualiﬁed contracts can be tax deferred and may 
reduce the client’s taxable income. In addiƟon, the interest or gain earned on contribuƟons is 
tax deferred. All distribuƟons from qualiﬁed contracts are considered taxable income. Currently, 
Securian Financial issues contracts under the following qualiﬁed plan types: • Roth Individual 
ReƟrement Annuity (Roth IRA) • Simpliﬁed Employee Pension (SEP) • TradiƟonal Individual 
ReƟrement Annuity (TradiƟonal IRA) • Inherited IRA ContribuƟons to non-qualiﬁed contracts are 
not tax deducƟble and do not reduce the client’s taxable income. The interest or gain earned on 
contribuƟons is tax deferred. The gain/interest is taxable upon distribuƟon. Currently, Securian 
Financial issues contracts under the following non-qualiﬁed plan types: • Non-Qualiﬁed • 
Inherited Non-Qualiﬁed • Corporate Non-Qualiﬁed Phases of AnnuiƟes There are two annuity 
phases: • The AccumulaƟon Phase: If a contract has not been annuiƟzed, it’s in the 
accumulaƟon phase. This is the phase during which clients make contribuƟons to the contract. 
In the accumulaƟon phase, contracts accumulate tax-deferred interest or gains. Immediate 
annuiƟes do not have an accumulaƟon phase since they are annuiƟzed within the ﬁrst year. • 
The Payout Phase: When a client annuiƟzes a contract, the payout phase begins. Clients begin 
receiving annuity income payments. For immediate annuiƟes, the payout phase begins within 
twelve months. Maturity Date The Internal Revenue Code requires that annuity contracts have 
an annuity start date, referred to in our contracts as the maturity date or annuity 
commencement date. If a contract does not provide for a reasonable maturity date, the IRS may 
not treat the contract as an annuity. This could result in severe tax penalƟes for contract 
owners. Contract owners must annuiƟze their contract in accordance with contract terms; 
however, in no event may annuiƟzaƟon begin later than the contract maturity date. If contract 
owners do not elect to annuiƟze prior to the maturity date, their contract will automaƟcally 
transfer to the payout phase and annuity payments will begin. Annuity Services will provide 
maturity noƟﬁcaƟon to contract owners and ﬁnancial professionals starƟng one year prior to 
the maturity date. 4 5 Account ConﬁrmaƟons and Statements An Annual Account Statement is 
provided to contract owners each January. The Annual Account Statement summarizes 
transacƟons completed on the contracts during the previous year. Variable contract owners will 
also receive quarterly statements. AnyƟme contract owners request a transacƟon that aﬀects 
the value of their contract, we will mail them a ConﬁrmaƟon of Account AcƟvity. The ﬁnancial 
professional may access their online Book of Business to view conﬁrmaƟons and statements for

which a decision can be made in the purchaser’s best interest. These regulaƟons oŌen include 
required disclosure(s) to the client along with replacement form(s) to be fully completed and

prompt delivery. 8 9 Rollover/Transfer/1035 Exchange The client may iniƟate the transfer of 
funds with the exisƟng company. If the client would like Annuity New Business to request the 
funds, the 1035 Exchange/Transfer/Rollover form must be submiƩed (please check with the 
ceding carrier(s) to verify whether they require their own forms). Rollovers What is a Rollover? 
In a rollover, the contract owner moves money from one type of IRA or qualiﬁed plan to another 
type of IRA or qualiﬁed plan. • In a direct rollover, the money is transferred directly between the 
two ﬁnancial insƟtuƟons, without passing through the hands of the contract owner. • In an 
indirect rollover, the owner receives the balance from the original insƟtuƟon and deposits it 
with a new insƟtuƟon within 60 days. Mandatory withholding may be applicable. Transfers 
What is an Asset Transfer? In a direct transfer, money from an IRA or qualiﬁed plan is 
transferred directly from one ﬁnancial insƟtuƟon to another and remains in the same type of 
IRA or qualiﬁed plan. In addiƟon, a SEP IRA to a TradiƟonal IRA would also be considered a 
direct transfer as SEP IRA and TradiƟonal IRA contribuƟons are treated the same way. Annuity 
New Business must request the funds directly from the ceding carrier(s). 1035 Exchanges What 
is a 1035 Exchange? A 1035 Exchange is the exchange of a non-qualiﬁed annuity contract, 
endowment or life insurance policy to a new or exisƟng non-qualiﬁed annuity contract with our

## Page 563

company. It is required that the registraƟon (annuitant(s) and owner(s) on the Minnesota Life or 
Securian Life applicaƟon) must be the same as the exisƟng contract that is being replaced. 
Annuity New Business must request the funds directly from the ceding carrier(s). A contract 
owner cannot do a 1035 Exchange from or to an annuity contract that has already been 
annuiƟzed. 1035 Exchange/Transfer/Rollover Tips Verify the exisƟng contract’s registraƟon to 
ensure an appropriate 1035 exchange. It is vital to set up the new contract with an acceptable 
registraƟon for the 1035 exchange to be completed. RegistraƟon entails that the 
owner/annuitant must be a “like to like” exchange with the same owner(s) and annuitant(s) on 
the exisƟng contract and the new applicaƟon. The ceding carrier may reject the transacƟon if 
the registraƟon is not the same. Verifying the correct contract registraƟon facilitates a prompt 
transfer of funds and saves addiƟonal Ɵme and paperwork. 10 Ensure the ceding carrier does 
not require their own paperwork. Many companies require the contract owner to sign their own 
forms prior to compleƟng the transacƟon. By ﬁrst checking to see if the other company requires 
their own paperwork, you can signiﬁcantly shorten the 1035 Exchange/Transfer/Rollover 
process. Conﬁrm whether the ceding carrier requires a Medallion signature guarantee. Many 
companies require that the signature of the contract owner be guaranteed for speciﬁc account 
types or purchase amount limits. Conﬁrm whether the ceding carrier requires an Oﬃcer’s 
Signature Many companies require the signature of an Oﬃcer of the receiving carrier to verify 
funds will be accepted. Provide an updated and complete address for the client’s current 
ﬁnancial services company. We rely on the address you provide on our 1035 
Exchange/Transfer/Rollover form as the correct address to send the paperwork. This step will 
ensure the paperwork is sent to the correct locaƟon and unnecessary delays are avoided. 
Replacement of our AnnuiƟes It is important to ensure that the customer has all the 
informaƟon needed to compare their current annuity’s features and beneﬁts with a new 
annuity. It is very important for the ﬁnancial professional to thoroughly document the reason 
why the replacement is appropriate for each individual client. Our company has an obligaƟon to 
do what is in the best interest of each client. We also need to comply with various regulaƟons. 
We also want to help you protect your exisƟng block of business and maintain high persistency. 
We realize that there are many factors involved in recommending a change in product and 
situaƟons can be unique. Our company has a long history of doing what is in the client’s best 
interest. Our policy coincides with others in our industry and was implemented to meet 
regulatory requirements and further enhance the overall service to the client. 10 11 12 Changes

contract owner. • The contract owner must also provide the percentage they want to transfer, 
the fund they are transferring from and the fund(s) they are transferring to. For informaƟon on 
the funds, see the applicable current prospectus. • All telephone transfers are recorded. 
Beneﬁciaries The beneﬁciary is an individual (or enƟty) designated by the contract owner to 
receive any proceeds payable following the death of the contract owner (or annuitant if owner 
is an enƟty). The beneﬁciary has no rights to the contract values during the contract 
owner’s/annuitant’s lifeƟme. As stated in the contract, if a beneﬁciary dies prior to the 
individual whose death triggers payment of the beneﬁt, that beneﬁciary’s interest in the 
contract ends with his/her death. The contract owner may change the beneﬁciary at any Ɵme;

## Page 565

irrevocable beneﬁciary designaƟons are not allowed. All beneﬁciary change requests must be 
made in wriƟng. It is preferable to use our Beneﬁciary Change Request form; however, any 
wriƩen request that includes the following informaƟon may be accepted: • Contract number • 
Contract owner’s name, signature and date of the signature • The beneﬁciary’s name and class 
• The relaƟonship of the beneﬁciary to the owner and some other type of idenƟfying 
informaƟon (date of birth, address or Social Security number) A contract owner may request 
that the death beneﬁt be paid in unequal shares to several beneﬁciaries. The shares must be 
indicated by percentages, not dollar amounts, since the value of the contract may change over 
Ɵme. The beneﬁciary change becomes eﬀecƟve when it is recorded in our Home Oﬃce records. 
AŌer the new designaƟon is recorded, it will take eﬀect as of the date the 14 contract owner 
signed the request. Note that any beneﬁciary designaƟon submiƩed will completely replace the 
previous designaƟon on ﬁle. AŌer the request to change the beneﬁciary has been processed, an 
“Acknowledgement of Beneﬁciary Change” is mailed to the contract owner and a copy is sent to 
the ﬁnancial professional. The contract owner should aƩach this document to the contract. You 
can ﬁnd the current beneﬁciary designaƟon on the eService Center, and on the client’s annual 
statement. If no beneﬁciary has been designated, the owner’s estate is the default beneﬁciary 
for most contract types. The following topics provide addiƟonal informaƟon about and common 
examples of beneﬁciary designaƟons. Children as Beneﬁciaries DesignaƟon of Children as 
Beneﬁciaries Children may be designated by name, for example, “James Doe, son, and Ann Doe, 
daughter.” Legally Appointed Guardians If a minor child is named as a beneﬁciary, and if the 
beneﬁciary is sƟll a minor at the Ɵme the claim request is submiƩed, we will not pay the minor 
beneﬁciary directly, since a minor cannot give a legally valid release for the payment. We will 
instead pay the proceeds to the custodian or guardian of the child. Court documentaƟon is 
generally required to indicate the individual who can serve as the custodian or guardian. Note 
that the custodian/guardian will not be determined unƟl the Ɵme of the claim, so the name of 
the custodian/guardian cannot be included in the beneﬁciary designaƟon. Per SƟrpes: Children 
of a Deceased Beneﬁciary A “per sƟrpes” designaƟon indicates that if that beneﬁciary 
predeceases the owner, the children of that beneﬁciary who are living at the Ɵme of the death 
of the owner will share equally in the share which their deceased parent would have taken if he 
or she had survived the owner. This designaƟon can be listed in the following way: “John Doe, 
per sƟrpes, and Jane Doe. per sƟrpes.” Under this designaƟon, if John is deceased at the Ɵme of 
the owner’s death, Jane Doe will be enƟtled to 50% of the proceeds from the contract, and John 
Doe’s living children will be enƟtled to share equally in the other 50% of the proceeds. Trust as 
Beneﬁciaries There are two general types of trusts: living trusts and testamentary trusts. With 
all trusts there is: • A grantor, usually the contract owner, the person who established the trust; 
• A trustee, who manages those assets as directed in the trust document; and • A beneﬁciary, 
for whose beneﬁt the trust was established. The contract owner may be both the grantor and a 
trustee. The trust may be either revocable (the grantor may “uncreate” or change the trust) or

## Page 566

irrevocable (the trust may not be revoked by the grantor). A “living trust,” also called an “inter 
vivos trust,” is a trust established by a living person. Assets are put into the trust and a trustee 
named to manage those assets. To name a trust as a beneﬁciary, the full name of the trust is 
required, including the date on which the trust was established. The trustees of the trust may 
not be named in the designaƟon. 14 15 An example of an acceptable trust designaƟon: • Bob 
Smith Revocable Trust dated March 1, 2020 Testamentary Trusts A “testamentary trust” is a 
trust created by the contract owner’s Last Will and Testament. It does not exist unƟl the 
contract owner dies and the will is probated. The Will itself may not be named as the 
beneﬁciary as the Will is not an enƟty that can collect death beneﬁt proceeds. Examples of 
acceptable testamentary trust designaƟons include: • “Trust created by my last will and 
testament” • “Bob Smith Trust created by my last will and testament” • “Testamentary Trust” 
SomeƟmes there will be two testamentary trusts created by the will, for example a Marital Trust 
and a Children’s Trust. In this case, the speciﬁc trust should be named in the beneﬁciary 
designaƟon. Examples of Common Beneﬁciary DesignaƟons If no class is indicated on the 
change request, it is assumed that all beneﬁciaries will be Class 1 (Primary) beneﬁciaries. If no 
percentage split is indicated, it is assumed that all beneﬁciaries in that class will share equally. • 
One beneﬁciary (Class 1/Primary): MARY DOE, Wife (not Mrs. John Doe, wife) • Two 
beneﬁciaries to share equally (both Class 1/ Primary): JOHN DOE, husband RICHARD DOE, son • 
More than two beneﬁciaries (both Class 1/Primary) with diﬀerent percentage splits: RICHARD 
DOE, son, 50% ROBERT DOE, son, 25% WILLIAM DOE, son, 25% • One primary beneﬁciary and 
two conƟngent beneﬁciaries who share equally: JOHN DOE, husband, Class 1 RICHARD DOE, 
son, Class 2 WILLIAM DOE, son, Class 2 • Estate: ESTATE (Do not include the name of the 
administrator or executor in the designaƟon as that role will be determined by the court during 
the probate process. • Charitable Beneﬁciaries: THE HOLY FAMILY CHURCH The current legal 
Ɵtle, address and tax idenƟﬁcaƟon number are required to idenƟfy the correct beneﬁciary at 
the Ɵme of claim. 16 Aliases and Name Changes (of the beneﬁciary) Aliases Occasionally a 
proposed beneﬁciary may go by more than one name. However, the full legal name is required

designaƟon. Predetermined Beneﬁciary Payout OpƟons Some contracts allow the owner to

directly from a foreign bank 18 19 Death Claim Procedures and OpƟons For all deferred (non-
annuiƟzed) contracts, the death beneﬁt is generally payable when the owner dies. If the owner 
is a non-natural enƟty, such as a Trust, the death beneﬁt is payable when the annuitant dies. For 
annuiƟzed contracts, the death beneﬁt is generally payable when the annuitant(s) dies. The 
opƟons available to a beneﬁciary depend on a number of factors, including the type of plan, 
type of contract, relaƟonship of the beneﬁciary to the owner, the value of the contract, and 
whether the contract is deferred or annuiƟzed. AddiƟonally, the claims process may be aﬀected 
by the aƩachment of a living beneﬁt rider to the contract. If you have quesƟons about whether 
a death beneﬁt is payable for a speciﬁc annuity, or what opƟons are available to a beneﬁciary, 
refer to the contract or contact Annuity Services. Annuity Death Claim OpƟons Deferred Annuity 
Contracts The following are opƟons that may be available to beneﬁciaries. Please discuss the 
contract with an Annuity Services associate prior to reviewing possible opƟons with the 
beneﬁciary. Lump-Sum Payment Death claim proceeds would be distributed via check or EFT in 
a single lump sum. When this opƟon is elected, all or part of the distribuƟon may result in 
taxable income to the beneﬁciary. AnnuiƟze the Contract The death claim proceeds would be 
converted into a stream of regular income payments. This opƟon may reduce the immediate tax 
burden by spreading the taxable gain over the life of the income stream. The amount and 
duraƟon of the payments depends on the annuity income opƟon and payment frequency 
selected. Note that once the annuiƟzaƟon opƟon is chosen, the beneﬁciary may no longer have 
the ability to withdraw addiƟonal money from the contract, and there may be addiƟonal 
restricƟons on his/her ability to make other changes to the arrangement. Under this opƟon, the 
Internal Revenue Code (IRC) requires the following: • For non-qualiﬁed annuity contracts: 
AnnuiƟzaƟon must be elected within 60 days of death, and income payments must commence 
prior to the ﬁrst anniversary of the date of death. • For all other annuity contracts: Income 
payments must commence prior to December 31 of the year following the date of death. Treat 
the contract as your own This opƟon is available only to sole spouse beneﬁciaries. The 
beneﬁciary would assume ownership of the contract and the tax-deferred status of the annuity 
will be maintained. ElecƟon of this seƩlement opƟon may require a plan type change to an 
Individual ReƟrement Annuity (IRA). 20 Rollover to an IRA This opƟon is available only to spouse 
beneﬁciaries. The death beneﬁt proceeds would be rolled over to an IRA in the beneﬁciary’s 
name. This opƟon would not result in a taxable event; however, the beneﬁciary would receive 
an IRS form 1099R reﬂecƟng the rollover. Establish a Beneﬁcial/Decedent Account with Securian 
This opƟon allows the beneﬁciary to retain the proceeds at Minnesota Life/Securian Life and 
substanƟally defer payment of income taxes. Under IRS provisions, the beneﬁciary will be

## Page 569

required to take a minimum distribuƟon each year and may take addiƟonal withdrawals as 
desired. In some circumstances, the beneﬁciary may be required to deplete the account in ten 
years. The amount of the “Required Minimum DistribuƟon (RMD)” is based on the beneﬁciary’s 
life expectancy and the required begin date for distribuƟon as is follows: • Non-qualiﬁed 
annuity contract: prior to the ﬁrst anniversary of the date of death. • All other annuity 
contracts: prior to December 31 following one year from the date of death. Please note, recent 
tax law changes have impacted the availability of this opƟon. Beneﬁciaries should consult with 
their tax advisor prior to elecƟng this opƟon. AnnuiƟzed Contracts If the contract has been 
annuiƟzed, the death beneﬁt remaining, if any, depends upon the annuity income opƟon 
chosen at commencement. If you have any quesƟons regarding the opƟons available to the 
beneﬁciary, please refer to the contract or contact Annuity Services. ReporƟng a Claim When 
you learn of a client’s death, please noƟfy Annuity Services immediately. This can be done by 
calling 1-800-362-3141 or sending an email to AnnuityClaims@Securian.com. When reporƟng a 
death, please provide the following informaƟon: • Name of the deceased annuitant/owner • 
Contract number(s) • Date of birth • Date of death • Name, address and phone number of the 
beneﬁciary(ies) and/or claim contract(s) Once noƟﬁed of a death, Annuity Services will send a 
leƩer and a claim form to the beneﬁciary. The leƩer will indicate the seƩlement opƟons 
available to the beneﬁciary, as well as what informaƟon will be required in order to seƩle the 
claim. A copy of this leƩer will be provided to the ﬁnancial professional. At the Ɵme of 
noƟﬁcaƟon, Annuity Services will also cancel any AutomaƟc Purchase Plans and SystemaƟc 
Withdrawals programs. 20 21 While the Claim is Outstanding While the claim is outstanding, 
Annuity Services will periodically aƩempt to make contact with the beneﬁciary to ensure we 
have accurate informaƟon on ﬁle. The beneﬁciary must retain contact with Annuity Services. If 
no opƟon has been elected within the Ɵme frame indicated by the contract and IRS guidelines, 
or we have lost contact with the beneﬁciary, the proceeds may be escheated to the appropriate 
state as unclaimed property. Once valid proof of death has been received at the home oﬃce, 
the death beneﬁt will be adjusted, if necessary, in accordance with the terms of the contract. If 
the contract is variable, the value of the contract will conƟnue to ﬂuctuate based on the 
performance of the investment accounts chosen by the contract owner. The beneﬁciary may 
elect to make adjustment to the allocaƟons, as allowed by the contract, by compleƟng a Sub-
Account Transfer Request form. If there are mulƟple beneﬁciaries, all beneﬁciaries must agree 
on the transfer request, as indicated by the signature of the ﬁnancial professional, and/or the 
signature of each beneﬁciary. While the claim is outstanding, the ﬁnancial professional will be 
copied on all correspondence sent to the beneﬁciary. SeƩling a Claim The following should be 
submiƩed for the seƩlement of every claim: • A legible copy of the death cerƟﬁcate as proof of 
death • A Beneﬁciary Claim Request form AŌer all necessary documentaƟon is received, the 
request will be processed, and a conﬁrmaƟon sent to the beneﬁciary, within ﬁve days of 
processing. Depending on the circumstances of the claim, addiƟonal documentaƟon may be

## Page 570

required. If you have any quesƟons during the claim process, please contact Annuity Services. 
AddiƟonal DocumentaƟon examples Estate as beneﬁciary: • The estate’s tax ID number • A 
cerƟﬁed copy of the court document naming the executor/administrator of the estate. 
Examples of this document are leƩers of administraƟon, leƩers testamentary, etc. Trust as 
beneﬁciary: • The trust’s tax ID number • CerƟﬁcaƟon of Trustee Authority form indicaƟng the 
individual(s) authorized to act on behalf of the trust 22 Minor beneﬁciaries: •May require court 
documentaƟon indicaƟng who may act on behalf of the minor. Beneﬁciary predeceased the 
contract owner: •Legible copy of the death cerƟﬁcate for the beneﬁciary. ElecƟon of 
annuiƟzaƟon opƟon: • Proof of the beneﬁciary’s age (for life conƟngency opƟons only). A copy 
of a driver’s license or birth cerƟﬁcate is acceptable. • AnnuiƟzaƟon request form and a valid 
payout quote. ElecƟon of beneﬁcial/decedent IRA at another company: • Transfer 
paperwork/acceptance from the other company. 22 23 DistribuƟons Contract owners can take 
various types of distribuƟons from their annuiƟes. AnnuiƟzaƟon DistribuƟon For immediate 
annuiƟes, annuiƟzaƟon must occur within one year of a contract owner’s ﬁrst annuity 
contribuƟon. Usually, immediate annuiƟes are annuiƟzed when the contract owner purchases 
the annuity. For deferred annuiƟes, immediate annuiƟzaƟon opƟons may vary by product. 
Immediate AnnuiƟzaƟon of your contract An immediate annuiƟzaƟon is when the ﬁrst annuity 
income payment is no later than one year aŌer the contact issue date. Submit the forms below 
to annuiƟze the contact along with the applicaƟon and condiƟonal forms that may be 
applicable. The following items are required for establishing an immediate annuity: • 
ApplicaƟon – must be signed by both the owner(s) and annuitant(s). • Annuity Payment and 
Withholding InstrucƟons – must be submiƩed to provide payment and withholding instrucƟons. 
• Annuity Quote - a copy must be submiƩed with the applicaƟon or annuiƟzaƟon request and 
within the guaranteed date stated on the quote. The informaƟon on the quote must match the 
applicaƟon or annuiƟzaƟon request form. • Proof of the Annuitant’s Age - required with a life 
conƟngency annuity opƟon. A copy of a driver’s license, birth cerƟﬁcate or passport is 
acceptable (if a joint opƟon is selected, submit proof of age for both annuitants). The following 
items are required for immediate annuiƟzaƟon of a deferred annuity: • Immediate 
AnnuiƟzaƟon Request • Annuity Quote – a copy must be submiƩed with the annuiƟzaƟon 
request and within the guaranteed date stated on the quote. The informaƟon on the quote 
must match the applicaƟon or annuiƟzaƟon request form. • Proof of the Annuitant’s Age – 
required with a life conƟngency annuity opƟon. A copy of a driver’s license, birth cerƟﬁcate or 
passport is acceptable (if a joint opƟon is selected, submit proof of age for both annuitants). 
AddiƟonal forms may be required depending upon jurisdicƟon state and replacement 
requirements, if applicable. Note: If the contract has any systemaƟc plans, we will automaƟcally 
stop the systemaƟc plans when annuiƟzaƟon occurs. 24 Annuity Income OpƟons At 
annuiƟzaƟon, clients choose an annuity income opƟon. Once established, annuity opƟons 
cannot change. Single Life Annuity The Single Life Annuity opƟon guarantees an income

## Page 571

throughout the lifeƟme of the annuitant. This annuity opƟon does not provide income or oﬀer 
beneﬁts to heirs or survivors. Period Certain Annuity A Period Certain Annuity opƟon provides 
income payments for a designated period of Ɵme – the period certain. If the annuitant dies 
before the end of the period certain, income payments will conƟnue to the beneﬁciary for the 
remainder of the period. Single Life Annuity with Period Certain The Single Life Annuity with 
Period Certain opƟon provides a guaranteed income throughout the lifeƟme of the annuitant 
with an addiƟonal guarantee that if the annuitant dies before the end of the period certain, 
income payments will conƟnue to the beneﬁciary for the remainder of the period. Joint Life 
with Survivor Annuity The Joint Life with Survivor Annuity opƟon provides income payments for 
as long as either annuitant is alive. This annuity opƟon will not provide income or oﬀer beneﬁts 
to heirs or survivors beyond the annuitant and joint annuitant. This opƟon is available in three 
forms: 1. Joint and Full – AŌer the death of either annuitant, the income payments conƟnue at 
the original amount. 2. Joint and 2/3 - AŌer the death of either annuitant, the income payments 
reduce to two-thirds of the original amount. 3. Joint and 1/2 - AŌer the death of either 
annuitant, the income payments reduce to one-half of the original amount. Joint Life with 
Survivor opƟons are also available with a period certain Single Life with Installment Refund 
Annuity The Single Life with Installment Refund Annuity opƟon guarantees an income 
throughout the lifeƟme of the annuitant. If at the annuitant´s death, the total income payments 
made are less than the amount annuiƟzed, income payments will conƟnue to the beneﬁciary 
unƟl the total income payments equal the amount annuiƟzed. (Not available for Variable 
AnnuiƟzaƟon.) Single Life with Cash Refund The Single Life with Cash Refund Annuity opƟon 
guarantees an income throughout the lifeƟme of the annuitant. If at the annuitant´s death, the 
total income payments made are less than the amount annuiƟzed, the diﬀerence is paid to the 
beneﬁciary in a lump-sum payment. (Not available for Variable AnnuiƟzaƟon.) What is the 
Amount of Each Payment? • The amount of each payment will vary from contract to contract. • 
Factors that aﬀect the payment amount include: a) Age, b) JurisdicƟon, c) Current Interest 
Rates, d) Annuity OpƟon, e) Amount AnnuiƟzed, f) Gender, and g) Plan Type. • The ﬁnancial 
professional is able to run an annuity quote that will illustrate the approximate payment 
amount. 24 25 AnnuiƟzaƟon Payment AllocaƟon OpƟons Fixed AnnuiƟzaƟon Fixed AnnuiƟzaƟon 
gives the client the same dollar amount for each payment. These payments are not subject to 
market risk as are the variable payments. Variable AnnuiƟzaƟon Variable annuiƟzaƟon allows 
for the client to choose the investment sub-accounts, which will fund their monthly annuity 
payments. The monthly annuity payments will ﬂuctuate with the performance of the chosen 
sub-accounts. The variable annuiƟzaƟon opƟon gives your client the opportunity to try to keep 
pace with inﬂaƟon. Assumed Investment Return (AIR) on Variable AnnuiƟzaƟon What is AIR? 
AIR is the Assumed Investment Return, also called the Assumed Interest Rate. When the ﬁrst 
payment is calculated, the variable annuity is assumed to perform at the AIR. This is important, 
as the AIR is the basis for how future variable annuity payments are calculated. Three things can

## Page 572

occur: • Investments perform below the AIR = Annuity payments decrease • Investments 
perform at the AIR = Annuity payments stay the same • Investments perform above the AIR = 
Annuity payments increase AIR Aﬀects Your Customer in Important Ways: Method by which the 
ﬁrst payment is calculated: The ﬁrst payment is calculated using the dollar amount to be 
annuiƟzed, the annuity income opƟon selected and the AIR. The amount to be annuiƟzed will 
be applied to purchase a number of annuity units based on the current annuity unit value for 
the applicable variable sub-account. The number of annuity units will not change (except at 
death when a reduced amount is paid to a survivor). The annuity unit value will change. Method 
by which all subsequent annuity payments are calculated: Subsequent payments are 
determined based on the investment performance of the underlying sub-account(s) and the 
amount by which the variable annuity investment account, net of expenses, has performed 
above or below the AIR. 26 Changes and TransacƟons aŌer AnnuiƟzaƟon Changes Once a 
contract is annuiƟzed, clients cannot change the annuity income opƟon, payment frequency or 
payment date. The client can make the following changes: • Name Change (i.e., marriage, 
divorce) • Address Change • Change Direct Deposit (Bank) InformaƟon • Withholding ElecƟon • 
Beneﬁciary Change (not available for Life Only opƟons) TransacƟons • Sub-account Transfer – 
available on variable annuiƟzaƟon only. Some contracts require a waiƟng period or minimum 
transfer amount. Refer to your contract prospectus for details. • Advance Withdrawal Beneﬁt – 
Available on the IncomeToday!® product only. Added to every contract where the state has 
approved the rider and the client selected an annuity opƟon that includes a period certain. 
Allows for a single withdrawal of a porƟon of the Withdrawal Value during the Withdrawal 
Period. Required Minimum DistribuƟon (RMD) What is a Required Minimum DistribuƟon? IRS 
guidelines for qualiﬁed annuity contracts state that contract owners must take a minimum 
distribuƟon from their annuity contracts once the annuitant reaches the required beginning 
date. These minimum distribuƟons must conƟnue every year aŌer that. ExcepƟon: • If the 
contract owner parƟcipates in a church or government plan, they may delay the RMD start date 
according to the plan document. Contact the plan administrator. How are the Contract Owner 
and RepresentaƟve NoƟﬁed? Annual Mailing We send a leƩer and an RMD systemaƟc 
withdrawal request form to the contract owner in the ﬁrst year that they are required to take an 
RMD. Annual Account Statement A message will print on the annual Account Statement for 
qualiﬁed plans. RMD NoƟﬁcaƟon Report A lisƟng is posted on our website under the Annuity 
Book of Business tool for each ﬁnancial professional during the ﬁrst quarter of each calendar 
year. The lisƟng contains all contract owners that are required to take a distribuƟon for the 
current calendar year. 26 27 How can Contract Owners Take a Required Minimum DistribuƟon? 
• Set up a systemaƟc withdrawal using the RMD SystemaƟc Withdrawal Request form. • 
Request a withdrawal from the contract. Contract owners need to request this each year. See 
Withdrawal from a Deferred Annuity for further informaƟon. • Complete and return a 
Telephone RedempƟon AuthorizaƟon Request. Once completed, this form allows the contract

## Page 573

owner to request a withdrawal over the phone. For the contract owner’s convenience, to 
ensure distribuƟon and avoid the IRS tax penalty that applies if the RMD isn’t taken, we 
recommend that they set up a systemaƟc withdrawal. What is the Amount of the Required 
Minimum DistribuƟon? The amount of the required minimum distribuƟon varies for each 
contract owner depending on the account balance and life expectancy elecƟon. The two life 
expectancy opƟons are: Single Life This opƟon uses the Uniform LifeƟme Table. Joint Life This 
opƟon uses the Joint Life and Last Survivor Table. To use this opƟon the only class I/primary 
beneﬁciary of the contract must be the contract owner’s spouse, and the spouse must be at 
least 10 years younger than the contract owner. The life expectancy opƟons listed above are not 
applicable for Beneﬁcial/Decedent Accounts. The total required minimum distribuƟon is based 
on all qualiﬁed contracts that the client may have, not just contracts held with us. However, 
RMD amounts for LIKE plans can be combined and distributed from just one company/contract. 
This is called an aggregate RMD. Using the RMD Calculator on our website, you can calculate a 
required minimum distribuƟon based on the contract owner’s qualiﬁed contracts. You may also 
contact Internal Sales Desk at 1-866-335-SELL (7355) for more informaƟon. When Must a 
Required Minimum DistribuƟon Be Taken? An RMD is a distribuƟon from a qualiﬁed reƟrement 
plan that must be taken when a parƟcipant reaches the required beginning date. The ﬁrst RMD 
is April 1, in the year following the year in which the parƟcipant reached the required beginning 
date. These minimum distribuƟons must be taken by December 31 each year following the 
iniƟal distribuƟon. SystemaƟc Withdrawals from a Deferred Annuity A systemaƟc withdrawal is 
a periodic withdrawal from an annuity contract. A systemaƟc withdrawal conƟnues unƟl the 
client chooses to stop it or unƟl either the contract’s value or sub-account value falls below the 
amount of the systemaƟc withdrawal. SystemaƟc withdrawals provide contract owners a way to 
receive money from their contract on a regularly scheduled basis. SystemaƟc withdrawals are 
only available from deferred annuiƟes. 28 Charges, Adjustments or PenalƟes for SystemaƟc 
Withdrawals ConƟngent Deferred Sales Charge (CDSC) There may be a charge for the 
distribuƟon. Please refer to the contract or prospectus for a breakdown speciﬁc to your contract 
type. The amount of the charge varies depending on the contract, how long the contract has 
been in force, the amount of the withdrawal, etc. Federal Income Tax PenalƟes DistribuƟons 
taken before age 59½ may be subject to federal income tax penalƟes. Our company does not 
withhold these penalƟes from the distribuƟon. To be excluded from the IRS premature 
distribuƟon penalty, the reason for the distribuƟon must qualify under the IRS guidelines (i.e., 
disability). Tax Sheltered AnnuiƟes (TSA) and Public Deferred CompensaƟon (457) plans will be 
subject to a 20% mandatory withholding if the contract owner doesn’t meet one of the 
qualifying reasons for distribuƟon. Our company does withhold this amount from the 
distribuƟon. Market Value Adjustment (MVA) MVA applies to distribuƟons from the guaranteed 
term account (GTA) prior to the renewal period in excess of the free amount. The MVA can be 
posiƟve or negaƟve. With a negaƟve MVA, there is a “ﬂoor” that guarantees the remaining

accumulaƟon value aŌer being reduced for any MVA, will not be less than contribuƟons less

## Page 577

This is what I was talking about the other day about income riders right now.  Many people use 
income riders as a ﬁxed income alternaƟve, especially since it’s guaranteed for life.  
  
ProtecƟve is oﬀering 7.35% at age 65, and it’s guaranteed for life.  For example, if you put in 
$100k you’d get $7,350/year for life.  Our SPIA at age 65 for a male client is roughly $7,700/year 
with no death beneﬁt, or $7,205/year for a cash refund death beneﬁt opƟon.  It’s hard to 
compete against income riders right now, because the guarantees are too strong.  
  
I ran some numbers and it seems like using Ultra for clients in their 70’s and 80’s for income can 
make sense, because there are no fees and they can withdrawal up to 10% free withdrawals and 
likely not deplete everything to $0 by the Ɵme they pass away.  For example, a 75 year old client 
pulling $8,500 on Ultra 7 can get up to 8.30% compound each year, but can pull up to 10% free 
withdrawals each year as well. 
  
Over the year I’ve told advisors with Ultra that they should pull the max free amount of income 
in years where the S&P 500 is negaƟve.  For example, why would you sell equiƟes at a major 
loss when you can sell your indexed annuity at 0%?  I’d argue you pull the max in downmarket 
years from Ultra, but you pull less in upmarket years.  This is where Ultra pairs well with 
equiƟes, and it’s a more strategic way of posiƟoning Ultra as a way to hedge against 
equiƟes.  Also, if longevity isn’t a key concern, why pay for income rider?  
  
I’ll give you a call in a bit to catch up if you’re free. 
 
Thanks, 
  
Rich Millis

Good morning! 
  
Jackson has added a new GMAB rider “Principal Guard” and has updated their M&E 
fee.  Jackson made an M&E charge reducƟon…the M&E fee reduces from 1.00% to .85% when 
the contract value is 250K or higher (based on quarterly anniversary values). 
  
Elite Access II is Jackson’s IOVA that competes with Momentum. Jackson does not have any 
index opƟons available like Momentum however they have hundreds of subaccounts vs roughly 
75 in Momentum. 
  
Another selling point with Jackson is their online trading capabiliƟes as of a few months ago 
momentum has online trading as well, I sƟll believe we can win some of this business if we get 
an advisor to focus on how much cheaper Momentum can be vs. Elite Access II….we beat them 
handily with the most popular subaccounts we use. 
  
1. American Funds Growth:  Jackson costs 91bps, Securian costs 53 bps. 
2. S&P 500 fund : Jackson costs 52bps, Securian costs 44bps

## Page 604

3. M&E fee:  Jackson is 1.00% for contracts under 250K, then .85% 250K+.  Securian is 
.90% under 1 million, then .80% above 1 million. 
4. Index opƟons…Jackson has none. 
  
BoƩom line we have selling points against Elite Access II, 38bps on AM Funds growth, 8bps on 
S&P 500 plus our index opƟons available.
